[tool-30b] exec_command <- Render each chess square as ASCII art label ($ python3 << 'EOF' from PIL import Image im = Image.open('/app/chess_board.png').convert('L') w,h = im.size sq = w//8 chars = " .:-=+*#%@" def render_square(sx, sy, scale=40): # sx, sy in square coordinates 0..7; y from top crop = im.crop((sx*sq, sy*sq, (sx+1)*sq, (sy+1)*sq)) crop = crop.resize((scale, scale)) pix = crop.load() lines=[] for y in range(scale): line='' for x in range(scale): v = pix[x,y] idx = int(v/256*len(chars)) if idx>=len(chars): idx=len(chars)-1 line+=chars[idx] lines.append(line) return lines # print square by square for all 64 for row in range(8): # y from top = rank from 8 to 1 for col in range(8): print(f"=== square col={col} row={row} (file={'abcdefgh'[col]}, rank={8-row}) ===") lines = render_square(col, row) for l in lines: print(l) EOF)

Actually, I recall there is a Python library `chess` and matplotlib images. But the images here...

Let me think about what piece set this is. The image proportions and piece designs:
- White pieces are rendered in "light" colors (bright, almost white fill with gray outlines).
- Black pieces are rendered in "dark" colors, but on light squares the black pieces appear as dark shapes with some light interior? Wait the ASCII for black pieces on light squares (e.g., a8) shows the piece as dark ('#','*','=','+') on light background. But the black pieces on dark squares (e.g., d8 queen, f8 bishop, g7 pawn, g5) are rendered as bright pixels on dark background. So the piece icons have outlines that make them visible on both backgrounds.

So the black pieces have a dark body with light outline maybe? Or the color scheme: it's like the "classic" unicode/letter pieces with dark outlines. Actually it looks like these are the "Cburnent" or "Mono" pieces?

Let me write a Python script that uses Unicode block characters (█,▓,▒,░) for better visualization. Or use PIL with 'L' and print each pixel as ' ' or '#' for below/above threshold? Piece shapes may be better visible with binary threshold: pixels darker than background => '#', else ' '.

Actually for black pieces on dark squares, the piece is *lighter* than background. So binary by "differs from local background" is best: mark pixels that differ significantly from the square's corner (background) color.

Let me implement: for each square, compute background as the color at corners (or the modal color). Then for each pixel, if |color - background| large, mark as piece. Then ASCII as '#' for piece, ' ' for background. That yields clean silhouettes. Let me do that for the uncertain pieces, at full 80x80.
[tool-8f5] exec_command <- Render binary silhouettes of uncertain pieces ($ python3 << 'EOF' from PIL import Image im = Image.open('/app/chess_board.png') w,h = im.size sq = w//8 def bg_color(col,row): # mode of corner pixels pts = [(col*sq+2,row*sq+2),(col*sq+sq-3,row*sq+2),(col*sq+2,row*sq+sq-3),(col*sq+sq-3,row*sq+sq-3)] vals=[im.getpixel(p) for p in pts] from collections import Counter return Counter(vals).most_common(1)[0][0] def sil(col,row): bg = bg_color(col,row) lines=[] for y in range(sq): line='' for x in range(sq): px = im.getpixel((col*sq+x, row*sq+y)) diff = sum(abs(px[i]-bg[i]) for i in range(3)) line += '#' if diff > 60 else ' ' lines.append(line) return lines names = {'a8':(0,0),'c8':(2,0),'d8':(3,0),'f8':(5,0),'f5':(5,3),'g5':(6,3),'c1':(2,7),'e2':(4,6),'c6':(2,2),'d5':(3,3)} out=[] for name,(c,r) in names.items(): out.append(f"=== {name} (bg={bg_color(c,r)}) ===") lines = sil(c,r) for l in lines: out.append(l.rstrip()) out.append('') open('/app/sil.txt','w').write('\n'.join(out)) print('done') EOF)

Let me generate: for each square crop (80x80), resize to 16x16, map to chars ' .:-=+*#%@'. Print row-blocks of 8 squares per rank. That gives 8 ranks * 16 rows = 128 lines; each line 8*16=128 chars + separators. Readable.
[tool-a3a] exec_command <- Render whole board as 16x16 per square ASCII grid ($ python3 << 'EOF' from PIL import Image im = Image.open('/app/chess_board.png').convert('L') sq = im.size[0]//8 chars = " .:-=+*#%@" def render_square(col,row): crop = im.crop((col*sq, row*sq, (col+1)*sq, (row+1)*sq)) crop = crop.resize((16,16)) pix = crop.load() grid=[] for y in range(16): line='' for x in range(16): v = pix[x,y] idx = int(v/256*len(chars)) if idx>=len(chars): idx=len(chars)-1 line+=chars[idx] grid.append(line) return grid outs=[] for row in range(8): blocks=[render_square(c,row) for c in range(8)] outs.append(f"----- rank {8-row} -----") for y in range(16): line=" | ".join(b[y] for b in blocks) outs.append(line) print('\n'.join(outs)) EOF)

[tool-113] exec_command <- Template matching silhouettes between board squares and piece references ($ python3 << 'EOF' from PIL import Image import numpy as np import os # Reference templates: load PNG, get alpha/light mask templates = {} for name in ['wk','wq','wr','wb','wn','wp','bk','bq','br','bb','bn','bp']: im = Image.open(f'/tmp/{name}.png').convert('RGBA') a = np.array(im) alpha = a[:,:,3] # mask of piece pixels (alpha > threshold) mask = (alpha > 120).astype(np.uint8) # also store rgb for color info: piece color = mean where mask rgb = a[:,:,:3] piece_rgb = rgb[mask>0] mean_color = piece_rgb.mean(axis=0) if len(piece_rgb) else np.array([0,0,0]) templates[name] = {'mask': mask, 'color': mean_color, 'is_white': name[0]=='w'} # our board board_img = Image.open('/app/chess_board.png').convert('RGBA') b = np.array(board_img) sq = 80 def bg_color(col,row): pts = [(col*sq+2,row*sq+2),(col*sq+sq-3,row*sq+2),(col*sq+2,row*sq+sq-3),(col*sq+sq-3,row*sq+sq-3)] vals=[tuple(b[y,x,:3]) for (x,y) in pts] from collections import Counter return Counter(vals).most_common(1)[0][0] def square_mask(col,row): bg = bg_color(col,row) mask = np.zeros((sq,sq), dtype=np.uint8) for y in range(sq): for x in range(sq): px = tuple(b[row*sq+y, col*sq+x, :3]) diff = sum(abs(px[i]-bg[i]) for i in range(3)) if diff > 60: mask[y,x]=1 return mask def iou(a,b): inter = np.logical_and(a,b).sum() union = np.logical_or(a,b).sum() return inter/union if union else 0 # Test on a8 (should be black rook) for (col,row,name) in [(0,0,'a8'),(5,0,'f8'),(2,0,'c8'),(3,0,'d8'),(5,3,'f5'),(6,3,'g5'),(2,2,'c6'),(1,1,'b7'), (0,7,'a1'),(7,7,'h1'),(4,7,'e1'),(4,6,'e2'),(2,7,'c1'),(0,5,'a3'),(2,5,'c3'),(1,6,'b2'),(5,6,'f2'),(6,6,'g2')]: m = square_mask(col,row) best=None; bestscore=-1 for tname, t in templates.items(): sc = iou(m, t['mask']) if sc > bestscore: bestscore=sc; best=tname # also compute second best for tie check print(f"{name}: best={best} score={bestscore:.3f}") EOF)

[tool-417] exec_command <- Compute piece pixel colors for classification ($ python3 << 'EOF' from PIL import Image import numpy as np from collections import Counter img = Image.open('/app/chess_board.png').convert('RGB') b = np.array(img).astype(int); sq=80 def bg_color(col,row): pts=[(col*sq+2,row*sq+2),(col*sq+sq-3,row*sq+2),(col*sq+2,row*sq+sq-3),(col*sq+sq-3,row*sq+sq-3)] return np.array(Counter([tuple(b[y,x]) for (x,y) in pts]).most_common(1)[0][0]) def piece_stats(col,row): bg=bg_color(col,row) patch=b[row*sq:(row+1)*sq, col*sq:(col+1)*sq] diff=np.abs(patch-bg).sum(axis=2) mask=diff>60 px=patch[mask] return mask.sum(), px.mean(axis=0), px.max(axis=0), px.min(axis=0) for (col,row,name) in [(0,5,'a3'),(1,6,'b2'),(5,6,'f2'),(6,6,'g2'),(2,5,'c3'),(0,7,'a1'),(7,7,'h1'),(4,7,'e1'),(4,6,'e2'),(2,7,'c1'), (0,0,'a8'),(3,0,'d8'),(5,3,'f5'),(1,1,'b7')]: n,mean,mx,mn=piece_stats(col,row) print(f"{name}: n={n} mean={mean.round(0)} max={mx.round(0)} min={mn.round(0)}") EOF)

[tool-18a] exec_command <- Connected component occupancy detection for all squares ($ python3 << 'EOF' import cv2 import numpy as np from PIL import Image from collections import Counter img = Image.open('/app/chess_board.png').convert('RGB') b = np.array(img).astype(int); sq=80 def bg_color(col,row): pts=[(col*sq+2,row*sq+2),(col*sq+sq-3,row*sq+2),(col*sq+2,row*sq+sq-3),(col*sq+sq-3,row*sq+sq-3)] return np.array(Counter([tuple(b[y,x]) for (x,y) in pts]).most_common(1)[0][0]) print(" a b c d e f g h") for row in range(8): line=f"{8-row} " for col in range(8): bg=bg_color(col,row) patch=b[row*sq:(row+1)*sq, col*sq:(col+1)*sq] diff=np.abs(patch-bg).sum(axis=2).astype(np.uint8) mask=(diff>60).astype(np.uint8) num, labels, stats, cents = cv2.connectedComponentsWithStats(mask, connectivity=8) # exclude background label 0, find components; piece = largest area areas = [] for i in range(1,num): areas.append((stats[i,cv2.CC_STAT_AREA], i)) if not areas: line+='. ' continue areas.sort(reverse=True) biggest_area = areas[0][0] # count all components > 80 (piece) and small ones (labels) big = sum(1 for a,i in areas if a>80) if big>0: ch = str(big) if big>1 else '#' line += ch + ' ' else: line += '. ' print(line) EOF)
